The book of the generation of Jesus Christ, son of David, son of Abraham. Abraham begat Isaac; Isaac begat Jacob; Jacob begat Judah and his brothers. Judah, however, begat Phares and Zara of Tamar, and Phares begat Esrom, and Esrom begat Aram; Aram [begat Aminadab]; Aminadab begat Nason; Nason begat Salmon; Salmon begat Booz of Rachab; Booz begat Obed of Ruth; Obed begat Jesse; Jesse begat David the king. David the king begat Solomon of the wife of Uriah; Solomon begat Roboam; [Roboam] begat [Abia; Abia] begat Asaph; Asaph begat Josaphat; Josaphat begat Joram; Joram begat Ozias; Ozias begat Joatham; Joatham begat Achaz; Achaz begat Ezechias; Ezechias begat Manasses; Manasses begat Amon;

Amon begot Josiah; Josiah begot Jehoiakim; Jehoiakim begot Jeconiah and his brothers in that captivity of Babylon. After that captivity of Babylon Jeconiah begot Shealtiel; Shealtiel begot Zerubbabel; Zerubbabel begot Abiud; Abiud begot Eliakim; Eliakim begot Azor; Azor begot Zadok; Zadok begot Achim; «Achim» begot Eliud; Eliud begot Eleazar; Eleazar begot Matthan; Matthan begot Jacob; Jacob begot Joseph the husband of Mary, of whom was born Jesus who they call Christ. All the generations from Abraham unto David are fourteen generations, and from David unto the captivity of Babylon are fourteen generations, and from the captivity of Babylon unto Christ are fourteen generations. Now the generation of Jesus Christ was thus, because his mother Mary was betrothed to Joseph and before they came together, she was found pregnant from the Holy Spirit. Joseph however, the husband of Mary, was righteous and did not want

to expose her; he decided to dismiss her secretly. And as he was thinking about this, behold an angel of the Lord was seen in a vision to him and said: "Joseph son of David, do not fear to take Mary your wife, because he who will be born of her is from the Holy Spirit; she shall bear a son and they shall call his name Jesus because he shall save his people from their sins." All this was done so that what was said by the Lord through the prophet might be fulfilled, which says "Behold a virgin shall conceive and shall bear a son and they shall call his name Emmanuel which is by interpretation God with us." Joseph awoke from sleep and did as that angel of the Lord commanded him. And he took his wife; and he did not know her until she bore a son, and they called his name Jesus.

They said to him: "In Bethlehem of Judea, for so it is written by the prophet: And you, Bethlehem, land of Judah, are by no means least among the princes of Judah, for from you shall come forth that great prince who shall shepherd my people Israel." Then Herod called those wise men secretly and ascertained from them the time of the appearance of that star; and he sent them to Bethlehem and said: "Go and search diligently for [that] child, and when you find him, report to me so that I [also] may go and worship him." When they had heard this from that king, they departed, and behold, that star which they saw in the east went before them until it came and stood where that child was; [and they], when they saw that star, rejoiced with very great joy, and they came to the house and saw that child with Mary his mother. and worshipped him and opened their treasures [and] offered him gifts, gold, frankincense, and myrrh. And they received a command in a vision not to return to Herod, but they escaped by another way and went into their own country. And when they had departed, behold, an Angel of the Lord appeared in a vision to Joseph and said: "Arise and take that child and his mother and flee into Egypt, and remain there until I tell you, for Herod is about to seek that child and destroy him." He arose and took that child and his mother by night and went into Egypt; and he was there until the death of Herod, that what was spoken by the Lord through the prophet might be fulfilled, which says: "Out of Egypt I called my son." Then Herod, when he saw that he had been tricked by those wise men, was very angry, and he sent and extirpated all the children who were in [Bethlehem and in all its borders from two years old and under, according to that time which he had ascertained from those wise men. Then was fulfilled that which was spoken by Jeremiah the prophet, who said: "A voice was heard from Ramah of lamentation

and weeping and great wailing; Rachel was weeping for her children and would not be comforted, because they are not." But when Herod had died, behold, an Angel of the Lord revealed himself in a vision to Joseph in Egypt; and he said: "Arise and take that child and his mother and go into the land of Israel; for they are dead who sought the life of this child." He arose and took that child and his mother and came into the land of Israel. And when he heard that Archelaus was reigning in Judea in place of his father Herod, he was afraid to go there, and he received a command in a vision and went into the region of Galilee. * And he came and dwelt (1) in that city which is called Nazareth; that what was spoken by the prophet might be fulfilled, that he shall be called a Nazarene.

of one crying in the desert; prepare the way of the Lord and make his paths straight." Now John himself was clothed in a garment of camel's hair and a leather belt about his loins: and his food was locusts and wild honey. Then Jerusalem and all Judea and the villages around the Jordan went out to him, and they were baptized by him in the Jordan and were confessing their sins. When he saw the Pharisees and Sadducees coming to be baptized by him, he said to them: "O brood of vipers, who has warned you to flee from that coming wrath? Produce therefore now fruit worthy of repentance; and do not think or say to yourselves, 'We have Abraham as our father.' For I say to you that God is able from these stones to raise up children to Abraham. For now behold, the axe lies at the roots of the trees: every tree that does not produce good fruit shall be cut down and cast into the fire. I baptize you with water for repentance; but he who comes after me is mightier than I, [whose] I am not worthy

Then Jesus came from Galilee to the Jordan to John to be baptized by him. But John was preventing him and saying: "It is I who need to be baptized by you, and you come to me?" Jesus answered and said to him: "Let it be now, for thus it is fitting for us to fulfill all righteousness." IV. - Then Jesus was led up by that Spirit into the desert to be tempted by the devil. And he fasted forty days and forty nights; and afterwards he was hungry. And that tempter came to him and said to him: "If you are the Son of God, say that these stones may become loaves of bread." But he answered and said: "It is written that not by bread alone does man live, but by every word that comes from the mouth of God." Then that devil took him into that holy city and set him upon the pinnacle of that temple, and said to him: "If you are the Son of God, throw yourself down from there, for it is written that he will command his angels concerning you, and on their hands they will lift you up, lest perhaps you strike your foot against a stone." the prophet Isaiah: "The land of Zebulun and the land of Naphtali, the way of the sea beyond the Jordan, Galilee of the Gentiles. The people who sat in darkness have seen a great light: and to those who sat in the regions and shadows of death, light has dawned." From then on Jesus began to preach and to say: "Repent, for the kingdom of heaven is at hand." And he was going along that seashore of Galilee; he saw two brothers, Simon who is called Peter and Andrew his brother; they were casting a net, for they were fishermen. And he said to them: "Come after me and I will make you fishers of men." They immediately left those nets and followed him. And he went on from there; he saw two other brothers, James of Zebedee «and» John his brother in a boat with Zebedee their father; they were mending their nets. And he called them; and they

them and said: "Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom [of heaven]. Blessed are those who mourn in heart, for they shall be comforted. Blessed are the meek, for they shall possess the earth. Blessed are those who hunger and thirst for righteousness, for they shall be satisfied. Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy. Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God. Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called children of God. Blessed are those who are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven. Blessed will you be when they persecute you and speak every evil word against you falsely for my sake. Rejoice and be glad, for your reward is great in heaven, for so also they persecuted the prophets who were before you. You are the salt of the earth. If that salt has lost its taste, how shall it be salted? It is good for nothing anymore (1) except to be thrown out and trampled underfoot by men.

You are the light of the world. A city built on a hill cannot be hidden, nor do they light a lamp and put it under a bushel, but on a lampstand, and it gives light to all who are in the house. Let your light so shine before men, that they may see your good works, [and glorify your Father in heaven. Do not think that I have come to abolish the law] or the prophets; I have not come to abolish, but to fulfill. For truly I say to you, until heaven and earth pass away, not one iota, not one horn will pass from the law and the prophets until all is accomplished. Whoever relaxes one of the least of these commandments and teaches men so, shall be called least in the kingdom of heaven; but whoever does them and teaches them, he shall be called great in the kingdom of heaven. For I say to you, unless your righteousness exceeds that of the scribes and

the Pharisees, you will never enter the kingdom of heaven. You have heard that it was said to those of old, 'You shall not murder.' And whoever murders will be liable to judgment. But I say to you that everyone who is angry with his brother without cause will be liable to judgment; whoever says to his brother, 'You fool,' will be liable to the council; and whoever says to his brother, 'You idiot,' will be liable to the Gehenna of fire. If you offer your sacrifice at the altar, and there remember that your brother has some evil heart against you, leave your sacrifice there before your altar and go first and be reconciled to your brother, and then come and offer your sacrifice. Agree with your adversary quickly, while you are still on the way with

him, lest your adversary hand you over to the judge, and the judge hand you over to the executioner, and they throw you into prison. Truly I say to you, you will not come out from there until you pay your last penny. You have heard that it was said, 'You shall not commit adultery.' But I say to you that everyone who looks at a woman to lust after her has already committed adultery with her in his heart. If your right eye causes you to sin, pluck it out and throw it away from you, for it is better for you that one of your members should perish, than that your whole body should be cast into Gehenna. And if your right hand causes you to sin, cut it off and throw it away from you, for it is better for you that one of your members should perish, than that your whole body should be cast into Gehenna. his wife, let him give her a certificate of divorce.' But I say to you that everyone who leaves his wife, except for the cause of fornication, makes her an adulteress; and whoever marries a divorced woman commits adultery. Again, you have heard that it was said to those of old, 'You shall not swear falsely, but shall perform your oaths to the Lord.' But I say to you: Do not swear at all, neither by heaven, for it is God's throne, nor by the earth, for it is his footstool, nor by Jerusalem, for it is the city of the great King, nor shall you swear by your head, because you cannot make one hair white or black; but let your speech be: 'Yes, yes,' and 'No, no'; anything more than this is from the evil one. You have heard that it was said: 'An eye for an eye and a tooth for a tooth'; but I say to you: Do not resist the one who is evil, but if

VI. - "But beware in your almsgiving that you do not do it before men in their sight, otherwise you will have no reward from your Father who is in heaven. When you do your alms, do not proclaim it before you, as the hypocrites do, in the synagogues and in the streets, that they may be honored by men, for they receive their reward from them. You,

And when you pray, do not be like the hypocrites, because they love to pray in the synagogues and streets so that they may be known to men: truly I say to you, that is their reward. But when you pray, enter into your treasury and close your door and pray to your Father in secret; and that Father of yours who sees in secret will reward you openly. darkness, and how great is that darkness? No one can serve two masters; for either he will hate the one and love the other, or he will be devoted to the one and despise the other. You cannot serve God and Mammon. Therefore I tell you, do not be anxious about your life, what you shall eat or what you shall drink, nor about your body, what you shall put on. Is not life more than food, and the body more than clothing? Look at the birds of the air: they neither sow nor reap nor gather into barns, and yet your heavenly Father feeds them. Are you not of more value than they? And which of you by being anxious can add one cubit to his span of life? And why are you anxious about clothing? Consider the lilies of the field, how they grow; they neither toil nor spin; yet I tell you, even Solomon in all his glory was not arrayed like one of these.

If God so clothes the grass of the field, which is here today and tomorrow is thrown into the oven, will he not much more clothe you, O you of little faith? Therefore do not be anxious, saying, 'What shall we eat?' or 'What shall we drink?' or 'What shall we wear?' For the Gentiles seek after all these things, and your heavenly Father knows that you need them all. But seek first the kingdom of God and his righteousness, and all these things will be added to you. Therefore do not be anxious about tomorrow, for tomorrow will be anxious for itself. Sufficient for the day is its own trouble."

or how can you say to your brother, 'Let me take the speck out of your eye,' when there is the log in your own eye? You hypocrite, first take the log out of your own eye, and then you will see clearly to take the speck out of your brother's eye. Do not give dogs what is holy, and do not throw your pearls before pigs, lest they trample them underfoot and turn to attack you. Ask, and it will be given to you; seek, and you will find; knock, and it will be opened to you. For everyone who asks receives, and the one who seeks finds, and to the one who knocks it will be opened. Or which one of you, if his son asks him for bread, will give him a stone? Or if he asks for a fish, will give him a serpent? If you then, who are evil, know how

to give good gifts to your children, how much more will your Father who is in heaven give good things to those who ask him! So whatever you wish that others would do to you, do also to them, for this is the Law and the Prophets. Enter by the narrow gate. For the gate is wide and the way is easy that leads to destruction, and those who enter by it are many. For the gate is narrow and the way is hard that leads to life, and those who find it are few. Beware of false prophets, who come to you in sheep's clothing but inwardly are ravenous wolves. You will recognize them by their fruits. Are grapes gathered from thornbushes, or figs from thistles? So, every healthy tree bears good fruit, but the diseased tree bears bad fruit.

Every tree that does not produce good fruit will be cut down and cast into the fire; therefore, by their fruits you shall know them. Not everyone who said to me, "Lord, Lord," shall enter into the kingdom of heaven, but he who does the will of my Father who is in heaven. Many will say to me on that day, "Lord, Lord, did we not prophesy in your name, and in your name cast out devils, and in your name perform many mighty works?" Then I will say to them, "I never knew you; depart from me, all you who work iniquity." And when he entered Capernaum, a centurion approached him, beseeching him, and saying, "Lord, my servant lies in my house paralyzed, grievously tormented." And Jesus said to him, "I will come and heal him." with you, Jesus, Son of God? Have you come here before the time to torment us?" And there was a herd of many swine feeding far from them. But those devils besought him, saying, "If you cast us out, command us to go into that herd of swine." And he said to them, "Go"; and they went into that herd of swine, and immediately that whole herd rushed from the cliff into the sea and perished in those waters. But those swineherds fled into the city and told everything, including about those demoniacs; and immediately that whole city came out to meet Jesus, and when they saw him, they besought him to depart from their borders.

And immediately certain of the scribes said in their hearts, "This man blasphemes"; and when Jesus understood that reasoning of theirs, he said to them, "Why do you think evil in your hearts? Which is easier - to say, 'Your sins are forgiven you,' or to say, 'Arise and walk'? these: first, Simon who is called Peter, and Andrew his brother; James of Zebedee and John his brother; Philip and Bartholomew; Thomas and Matthew the tax collector; James of Alphaeus and Lebbaeus who is called Thaddaeus; Simon the Cananaean and Judas Iscariot who also betrayed him. These twelve Jesus sent out; he commanded them and said, "Do not go into the way of the Gentiles, and do not enter into a city of the Samaritans; go rather only to the lost sheep of the house of Israel. And as you go, preach and say that the kingdom of heaven has drawn near; heal the sick; cleanse the lepers, cast out devils; freely you have received, freely give. Do not provide gold or silver or copper in your belts, nor a bag for the journey, nor two tunics, nor sandals, nor a staff, for the laborer is worthy of his food. And when you enter into a house, greet it and say, "Peace to this house:" and if that house is worthy, let that peace of yours come upon it; if it is not worthy, let that peace of yours return to yourselves. Truly I say to you, it will be more tolerable for the land of Sodom and Gomorrah in the day of judgment than for that city. Behold, I send you out as sheep among wolves. and in their streets they will scourge you; and before governors and kings they will present you for my sake as a testimony to them and to the Gentiles. But when they deliver you up, do not be anxious about how or what you should speak, for it will be given to you in that hour what you should speak; for it is not you who speak, but that Spirit of your Father who speaks within you. For brother will deliver brother to death, and father his child, and children will rise up against their parents and put them to death; and you will be hated by all for my name's sake; but he who endures to the end, he shall be saved. But when they persecute you in this city, flee to another; if they cast you out from there also, flee from there to a second. Truly I say to you, you will not have finished the cities of Israel until the coming of the Son of Man.

A disciple is not above his teacher, nor a servant above his master: it is enough for the disciple if he be as his teacher, and for that servant as his master. If they have called the master of the house Beelzebul, how much more those of his household. Do not fear them: for there is nothing hidden that will not be revealed, nor any mystery that will not appear. What I tell you in the darkness, speak in the light, and what you hear in the ears, preach it upon the housetops. And do not fear those who will destroy your flesh, but cannot destroy your souls; but rather fear him who is able to destroy both soul and flesh in Gehenna. Father? But even the hairs of your head are all numbered; do not fear therefore, for you are of more value than many sparrows. Everyone who confesses me before men, I also will confess him before my Father who is in heaven; but whoever denies me before men, I also will deny him before my Father who is in heaven. Do not think that I have come to bring peace upon the earth; I have not come to bring peace, but a sword. For I have come to divide a man against his father, and a daughter against her mother, and a daughter-in-law against her mother-in-law, and a man's enemies will be those of his own household. He who loves his father or his mother more than me is not worthy of me; and he who loves his son or his daughter more than me is not worthy of me. And he who does not take up his cross and follow me, he

Truly I say to you, there has not arisen among those born of women one greater than John the Baptist; but he who is least in that kingdom of heaven is greater than he. And from the days of John the Baptist until now, the kingdom of heaven suffers violence, and the violent take it by force. For all the prophets and the law prophesied until John, and if you are willing to receive it, this is Elijah, he who is to come. He who has ears to hear, let him hear. At that time Jesus spoke and said, "We confess to you, Father, Lord of heaven and earth, because you have hidden these things from the wise and prudent and have revealed them to babes; yes, Father, for so it was pleasing before you. All things have been delivered to me by my Father, and no one knows the Son except the Father, nor does anyone know the Father except the Son and he to whom the Son wills to reveal him. Come to me, all you who are weary and have carried a heavy burden, and I will refresh you: take my yoke upon you and learn from me, for I am meek and lowly in heart, and you will find rest for your souls, for my yoke is easy and my burden is light."

"Have you not read what David did, when he and those who were with him were hungry, how he entered into the temple of God, and ate those loaves of the sacrifices, which it was not lawful for him to eat, nor for those who were with him, but for the priests only? Or have you not read the law, that on the Sabbath the priests in that temple neglect the Sabbath and are innocent? But I say to you that one greater than the temple is here. If you had known what this is: 'I desire mercy and not sacrifice,' you would not have condemned the innocent, for the Son of Man is Lord even of the Sabbath." And Jesus went from there and came into their synagogue; and there was a man there whose hand was withered. And they questioned him and said if it is lawful to heal on the Sabbath, that they might accuse him. But Jesus said to them, "Who is the man among you who has one sheep, and if it falls into a pit on the Sabbath, will he not take hold of it and lift it out? Now therefore

is, how then will his kingdom operate? If I cast out demons by Beelzebub, by whom do your sons cast them out? Therefore they will be your judges. If I cast out demons by the Spirit of God, then that kingdom of God has come upon you. Or how can anyone enter the house of a strong man and plunder his vessels unless he first binds that strong man, and then indeed he will plunder his house. He who is not with me is my enemy, and he who does not gather with me, scatters. For this reason I say to you: "Every sin and blasphemy will be forgiven to men; but blasphemy against the Spirit will not be forgiven. that tree bad and its fruit bad, for the tree is known by its fruit. Brood of vipers, how can you speak good, because you are evil; for out of the abundance of the heart the mouth speaks. And while he was still speaking to that crowd, behold, his mother and his brothers stood outside and were seeking to speak to him. Then someone said to him: "Behold, your mother and your brothers are standing outside and want to speak something to you." But Jesus answered the one who told him and said: "Who is my mother or who are my brothers?" And he stretched out his hand to his disciples and said: "Behold my mother and my brothers: for whoever does the will of my Father in heaven, he is my brother, sister, and mother."

much in parables and said: "Behold, a sower went out to sow, and in that sowing of his, some fell by the wayside, and the birds of the air came and ate it; and other fell on rock where there was not much earth, and immediately it sprang up, because there was no depth of earth; the sun rose and scorched it; because it did not push out roots, it withered. And that other fell among thorns, and the thorns grew up and choked it. And some fell on good ground and yielded fruit, some a hundredfold, some sixty, and some thirty. He who has ears to hear, let him hear." And the disciples approached and said to him: "Why do you speak to them in parables?" But Jesus said to them: "Because it has been given to you to know the mystery of the kingdom of heaven; but to them it has not been given. For whoever has, to him it will be given and he will have abundance; and whoever does not have, even what he has will be taken away from him. For this reason I speak to them in parables,

because seeing they do not see and hearing they do not hear, [and] neither do they understand; and the prophecy of the prophet Isaiah is fulfilled upon them, which said: "Hearing you will hear and will not understand, and seeing you will see and will not see; for the heart of this people has grown dull and they have heard heavily with their ears; and their eyes are closed, lest perhaps they see with their eyes and hear with their ears and understand with their heart and turn and be healed." But your eyes are blessed, because they see; and your ears, because they hear. Amen I say to you, that many prophets and righteous men desired to see what you see and could not see, and to hear what you hear and could not hear. You now hear the parable of the sower. Everyone who hears the word of the kingdom and does not understand, that evil one comes and snatches away what was sown in his heart. This is he who was sown by the wayside. But he who was sown on good ground, this is he who hears that word and understands and produces fruit and yields some a hundredfold, some sixty, some thirty." tares among the wheat and went away. When therefore the herb sprouted and produced fruit, then indeed those tares appeared. And those servants of the master of the house came and said to him: "Master, did you not sow good seed in this field of yours? Now from where are these tares added?" But he said to them: "An enemy man did this." Those servants said to him: "If you wish, let us go and root them out." But he said to them: "No, lest perhaps in rooting out that tare you also root out the wheat with them; let both grow together until the harvest, and at the time of harvest I will command the reapers: "Gather the tares first and bind them in bundles to be burned, but gather that wheat into my barn." He was telling them another parable and said: "The kingdom of heaven is like

He was telling them another parable: "The kingdom of heaven is like leaven, which a woman takes and mixes into three measures of flour until it has leavened everything." All this Jesus spoke to that crowd in parables, and without a parable he spoke nothing to them, so that that word spoken by the prophet might be fulfilled: "I will open my mouth in parables and I will speak things hidden from the foundation of the world." of the tares and the field." He answered them and said: "He who sows that good seed is the Son of Man. The field is this world; that good seed, these are the sons of the kingdom, and those tares are the sons of the evil one. But that enemy who sowed them is the devil, and that harvest is the end of this age, and those reapers are the angels. As they gather the tares and burn them with fire, so it will be at the end of this age. The Son of Man will send his angels and they will gather out of his kingdom all temptations and those who do iniquity, and will cast them into that furnace of burning fire; there will be weeping and gnashing of teeth. Then the righteous will shine forth like the sun in the kingdom of heaven; he who has ears to hear, let him hear. Again, the kingdom of heaven is like a treasure hidden in a field, which a man found and hid, and for that joy he went away, and sold everything he had and bought that field. Again, the kingdom of heaven is like a merchant man, who seeks

They said to him: "Yes, master." But he said to them: "For this reason every scribe instructed in the kingdom of heaven is like a man, a master of a house, who brings forth from his treasure new and old." And it was when Jesus had finished these parables, he went away from there; and he came into his own region, and he was teaching them in their synagogues. And they were amazed and were saying: "From where is this wisdom of his and these powers? Is this not the son of that carpenter? Do they not call his mother Mary and his brothers James and Joseph and Simon and Judas, and his sisters, are they not all with us? From where is all this of his?" And they were offended at him. But Jesus said to them: "A prophet is not despised except in his own region and in his own house;" and he did not do many powers there because of their unbelief.

XIV. - At that time Herod the Tetrarch heard the fame of Jesus and said to his servants: "This is John the Baptist: he has risen from the dead, and for this reason those powers are performed by him." For Herod had then apprehended John, shackled him, and shut him in prison because of Herodias, the wife of Philip his brother; for John was saying to him: "It is not lawful for you to join yourself with her." And he wanted indeed to kill him, but he feared that crowd, because they regarded him as a prophet. But when the birthday of Herod occurred, the daughter of Herodias danced in the midst, and that dancing of hers pleased Herod; from whom with an oath he promised to give her whatever she might ask. But she was previously instigated by her mother, and said: "Give me here on a platter the head of John

And he sent and beheaded John in the prison; and they brought his head on a platter and gave it to that girl, and she brought it to her mother. And his disciples came and took away his corpse and buried it and came and reported to Jesus. But when Jesus heard, he withdrew from there by boat and went from there into a desert place privately. And as that crowd heard, they followed him on foot from the cities; and Jesus went out and saw a great crowd and had compassion on them and healed their souls. And as it was evening, his disciples approached and said to him: "This place is desert and the hour has passed; dismiss this crowd so that they may go around into the villages and buy themselves food." that wind was contrary. And in the fourth watch of the night Jesus came to them walking. But the disciples as they saw him when he was walking on the sea were troubled and said that it is some phantom. And for that fear they cried out; immediately Jesus spoke to them and said: "Be firm; it is I; do not fear." Peter answered and said to him: "Master, if it is you, command me to come to you on these waters." But he said: "Come." And Peter descended from the boat and was walking on the waters and went to Jesus; and as he saw that strong wind, he feared and began to sink. And he cried out and said: "Master, save me!" Immediately Jesus stretched out his hand, caught him and said: "O of little faith, why did you doubt?" And as they ascended into that boat, that wind ceased. But those who were in that boat approached and worshipped him and were saying: "Truly you are the Son of God." XV. - Then Pharisees and scribes from Jerusalem approached Jesus and said: "Why do your disciples transgress that tradition of the elders; for they do not wash their hands when they eat bread?" He answered and said: "Why do you also transgress the commandment of God because of your tradition? For God said: "Honor father and mother, and whoever curses father or mother, let him die the death." But you say: "Whoever says to his father or mother, with a gift it will profit you nothing from me;" and he will not honor his father, and you have made void the word

But what goes out through the mouth comes out from the heart; that defiles a man; for out of the heart come evil thoughts - murders, adulteries, fornications, thefts, false testimonies, blasphemies; these are what defile a man. But to eat with unwashed hands does not defile a man." And Jesus went out from there and came into the regions of Tyre and Sidon; and a Canaanite woman went out from their borders, was crying out and saying: "Have mercy on me, Master, son of David, because my daughter is badly demonized." were walking and the maimed were healed and the blind were seeing, and they were glorifying the God of Israel. But Jesus called the disciples and said to them: "I have compassion on this crowd, because now behold it is the third day since they have been waiting for me, and they have nothing to eat, and I do not want to dismiss them fasting, lest perhaps they fall on the way." But his disciples said to him: "From where is there for us in the desert so much bread to satisfy such a crowd?" But Jesus said to them: "How much bread do you have here?" But they said: "Seven loaves and a few fish." And he commanded that crowd to sit on the ground. And he took those seven loaves and the fish and was giving thanks, and broke and was giving to the disciples; but those disciples were giving to that crowd. And they all ate and were satisfied; and they took up those leftover fragments, seven baskets full;

XVI. - And the Pharisees and Sadducees approached him: they tempted and asked him to show them a sign from heaven. But he himself answered and said to them: "When it becomes evening, you say: "It will be fair, because the sky is red;" and in the morning you say: "There will be a storm, because the sky is sadly dimmed." Hypocrites, you know indeed how to discern the face of the sky, but you could not understand the signs of the times? An evil and adulterous generation seeks a sign, and no sign will be given to it except that of the prophet Jonah." And he left them and went away. And his disciples came and forgot to take bread. But Jesus said to them: "Beware and be cautious of that leaven of the Pharisees and Sadducees." But they were thinking in their hearts and were saying

Jesus answered and said to him: "Blessed are you, Simon, son of Jonah, because flesh and blood have not revealed to you, but my Father, who is in heaven. And I also say to you that you are Peter and upon this Rock I will build my church, and the gates of hell cannot prevail against it. And I will give you the keys of the kingdom of heaven. And whatever you bind on earth will be bound in heaven, and whatever you loose on earth will be loosed in heaven." And when they came to that crowd, a certain man approached him, knelt down, and said: "Lord, have mercy on my son, for he is a lunatic (?) and is severely tormented, for he often falls into the fire and often into the water; and I brought him to your disciples, and they could not heal him." Jesus answered and said to them: "O faithless and perverse generation, how long shall I be with you? How long shall I bear with you? Bring him here to me." And Jesus rebuked him, and the demon went out of him, and the boy was healed from that hour.

and become like children, you will not be able to enter the kingdom of heaven; for whoever humbles himself like this child, he is the greatest in the kingdom of heaven; and whoever receives one such child in my name, receives me; but whoever causes one of these little ones who believe in me to stumble, it would be better for him if a millstone were hung around his neck and he were cast into the depths of the sea. Woe to this world because of temptations, for temptation must come; but woe to that man by whom the temptation comes. If your hand or your foot causes you to stumble, cut it off and throw it from you; it is better for you to enter into life maimed or without a hand than to have two hands or two feet and fall into eternal fire. And if your eye causes you to stumble, pluck it out and throw it from you. It is better for you

heaven; for where two or three are gathered in my name, I am there among them." Then Peter approached him and said: "Lord, how often will my brother sin against me and I forgive him? Up to seven times?" Jesus said to him: "I do not say to you up to seven times, but up to seventy times seven. Therefore the kingdom of heaven is likened to a king who wanted to settle accounts with his servants: and as he began to settle, one debtor of ten thousand talents was brought to him. And since he had nothing to pay, that lord commanded him to be sold, along with his wife, children, and all that he had, and the debt to be collected. The lord of that servant had compassion, released him, and forgave him that debt. And as that servant went out, he found one of his fellow servants who owed him a hundred drachmae: he seized him and choked him and said: "Give me whatever you have." That fellow servant fell down and begged him and said: "Be patient with us, and I will pay you." But he would not, but went and threw him into prison until he should pay that debt. When therefore his fellow servants saw what had happened, they were very saddened, and they came and reported all that had happened to their lord. He called

Jesus said to him: "If you wish to be perfect, go and sell your possessions and give to the poor, and you will receive treasure in heaven; and come and follow me." But when that young man heard this word, he went away sorrowful; for he was very rich. Jesus then said to his disciples: "Truly I say to you, it is difficult for a rich man to enter the kingdom of heaven. Again I say to you, it is easier for a rope to pass through the eye of a needle than for a rich man to enter the kingdom of God." When his disciples heard this, they were greatly amazed and said: "Who then can be saved?" Jesus looked at them and said:

XX. - "For the kingdom of heaven is like a master of a house who went out at dawn to hire laborers for his vineyard. And he made an agreement with those laborers for a stater each for the day, and sent them into his vineyard. And he came about the third hour, and saw others standing idle in the marketplace; and he said to them: "Go you also into that vineyard of mine, and whatever is right I will give you;" and they went. Again he went out at the sixth and at the ninth hour and did likewise. But at the eleventh hour, when he went out, he found others standing and said to them: "Why do you stand here all day idle?" They said to him: "Because no one has hired us." He said to them: "Go you also into

And Jesus entered the temple and drove out all the sellers and buyers in that temple and overturned the tables of the money changers and the chairs of those who sold doves. And he said to them: "It is written: 'My house shall be called a house of prayer'; but you have made it a den of robbers." And the blind and the lame approached him in that temple, and he healed them. When those chief priests and scribes saw those wonderful things that he did and those children who were crying out in that temple and saying: "Glory to the Son of David," they were indignant; and they said to him: "Do you hear what these are saying?" Jesus said to them: "Have you not read that out of the mouth of infants and nursing babes you have perfected praise?" And he left

them and went outside the city to Bethany and was there. In the morning as he was going up to the city, he was hungry and saw a fig tree by the road, and he came to it and found nothing on it except leaves only. And he said to it: "May no fruit ever come from you forever;" and that fig tree withered immediately. When the disciples saw it, they were amazed and said: "How did this fig tree wither immediately?" Jesus answered and said to them: "Truly I say to you, if you have faith and do not doubt, you will not only do the miracle of this fig tree, but even if you say to this mountain: 'Rise and fall into the sea,' it will be. Jesus said to them: "Have you never read in the Scripture: 'This stone which the builders considered as nothing, that has become the head of the corners'? This was from the Lord, and it is wonderful before our eyes. Because of this I say to you that the kingdom of God will be taken from you, and will be given to a nation that produces its fruits; and whoever falls upon that stone will be broken, and upon whom it falls, it will crush him." XXIII. - Then Jesus was speaking to that crowd and his disciples; and he said: "Upon the thrones of Moses have sat the scribes and the Pharisees; all things whatsoever they say to you, do and observe. But according to their works do not do; for they say and do not do. They bind heavy and unbearable burdens, and impose them upon the shoulders of men: but they themselves do not want to move them with their finger. All their works they do in the sight of men; they make their garments wide, and make their fringes long. They love the first reclining at dinner and the first seating

Woe to you, scribes and Pharisees hypocrites, because you go into the sea and onto dry land to convert one person; and when you have converted him, you make him a child of Gehenna twice as much as you. Woe to you, blind scribes, because you say: 'Whoever swears by the temple, it is nothing; but whoever swears by that gold which is in that temple, it is binding.' You fools and blind, what is greater, the gold or that temple which sanctifies that gold? And whoever swears by the altar, it is nothing, but whoever swears by that offering which is upon it, it is binding. Woe to you, scribes and Pharisees hypocrites, because you tithe mint, dill, and cumin, and have left the better things in the law, justice and mercy and faith; these it was fitting for you to do and not to leave those. Woe to you, scribes and Pharisees hypocrites, because you have likened yourselves to whitewashed tombs which from the outside appear beautiful, but inside are full of the bones of the dead and all uncleanness. So also you from the outside appear to men as fasting, but inside you are full of deceit and iniquity. Woe to you, scribes and Pharisees hypocrites, because you build those tombs of the prophets and adorn those tombs of the just; and you say: 'If we had been in those days of our fathers, we would not have associated ourselves with them in that blood of the prophets.' Now you are witnesses to yourselves that you are the children of those who exterminated the prophets. And you also have filled the measure of your fathers.

You serpents, children of vipers, how will you flee from the judgment of Gehenna? Because of this, behold, I have sent to you prophets and wise scribes: some of them you killed and crucified, and some of them you whipped in your synagogues and persecuted from city to city; so that there might come upon you all the just blood shed upon the earth from the blood of Abel the just to the blood of Zechariah the son of Baruch, whom you killed between that temple and the altar. Truly I say to you, that all this will come upon this generation. Jerusalem, Jerusalem, which destroyed the prophets and stoned your apostles, how often I wanted to gather your offspring as a hen gathers her chicks under her wings, and you did not want to. Behold, your house is left

For as lightning when it shines in the east of the sun and shines even to the west, in this way will be the coming of the Son of man. For wherever the corpse will fall, there also the eagles will be gathered. Immediately after that tribulation of those days, the sun will be darkened and the moon will not give its light, and the stars will fall from heaven and the powers of the heavens will be moved. And then that sign of the Son of man will appear in heaven and all the generations of the earth will lament. And they will see the Son of man coming upon the clouds of heaven in power and great glory. And he will send his angels with a great trumpet and will gather his elect from the four winds from the side

of the heavens to their side. But from the fig tree learn this parable: because when its branches become tender and it sprouts a leaf, you will know that the spring is near. In this way also you, when you see all this, you will know that it is near, at the doors. Truly I say to you, this generation will not pass away until all this happens. Heaven and earth will pass away; but my words will never pass away. But that day and time no one knows, neither the angels in the heavens nor the Son, but the Father only. As those days of Noah, in this way will be that coming of the Son of man; for as they were in those days before the flood; they were eating and drinking, marrying and being married until then to the day on which Noah entered the ark; and they did not know until that flood came and took away all. So will be that coming

of the Son of man. Then two will be in the field, one they will take and one they will leave; two will be grinding wheat: one they will take and one they will leave. Be vigilant because you do not know on what day that Lord of yours will come. Know that if the master of the house knew at what hour the thief was going to come, he would not allow his house to be dug through. Because of this, you also be prepared, because at the hour you will not think, the Son of man will come. Who then is that faithful and prudent servant whom the lord has set over his household to give them food in time? Blessed will be that servant to whom his lord will come and find him doing so. In the middle of the night a cry was made: "Behold the bridegroom! Go out and meet him." Then all those virgins rose and trimmed those lamps of theirs. But those foolish ones were saying to those prudent ones: "Give us also oil of this oil of yours, because our lamps are going out for us." Those prudent ones answered and said: "Lest perhaps it may not be enough for us and you, go to the sellers and buy for yourselves." When they went to buy, that bridegroom came, and those who were prepared entered with him to the wedding, and the door was closed. And he went a little further and fell upon his face, and prayed and said, "My Father, if it is opportune, let this cup pass from me; yet not as I will, but as you [will]." And he came to the disciples and found them sleeping and said to Peter, "So, you could not watch with me one hour? Watch and pray that you do not enter into temptation: the spirit is eager, but the flesh is weak." Again a second time he went, prayed, and said, "My Father, if it is not possible for this cup to pass from me, that I should not drink it, your will be done." And he came again and found them sleeping, for their eyes were heavy; and he left them. And he went and prayed a third time, saying the same words. In that hour Jesus said to that crowd, "As against a robber you have come out with sword and with club to seize me; daily I sat in that temple with you and taught you, and you did not seize me. And they bound him and led him away and delivered him to Pilate the judge. Then Judas his betrayer saw that he was condemned, he repented and gave back that silver to the high priests and elders; and he said, "I have sinned, for I have betrayed innocent blood." But they said, "What is that to us? You see to it." And he threw that silver into that temple and went away and hanged himself. But the high priests took that silver and said, "It is not fitting to put this into the treasury, for it is the price of blood." They took counsel and bought with it that potter's field for the burial of strangers; for this reason that field is called the field of blood to this present day. Then was fulfilled the word

Pilate said to them, "You have a guard; go and guard it as you know." But they went and guarded that sepulcher and sealed that stone with the guard. XXVIII. - In the evening of the Sabbath, which was dawning into the first of the Sabbath, came Mary Magdalene and the other Mary to see that sepulcher. And behold, there was a great earthquake, for an angel of the Lord descended from heaven, came and rolled back that stone from that door and sat upon it. His appearance was like lightning and his garment was white as snow; and for fear of him those guards trembled and became as dead men. That angel answered and said to those women, "Do not be afraid; I know that you seek Jesus who was crucified; he is not here, but he has risen.

ჰრქუეს: ესრეთ თქუთ ვითარმედ მოწაფენი მისნი ღამე მოვიდეს და წარიპარეს იგი ვიდრე ჩუენ მეშინა· ” დაღათუ ესმეს ესე ბჭესა' მას. ჩუენ ვარწმუნოთ მას და თქუენ უზრუნველ გყვნეთ ” ხოლო მათ მიიღეს ვეცხლი იგი. და ყვეს ეგრე" ვითარცა ”ისწავეს მათ გან. და განითქუა სიტყუაი ესე ჰურიათა შორის ვიდრე დღენდელად დღემდის" ” ხოლო ათერთმეტნი იგი მოწაფენი წარვიდეს გალილეას' მთასა მას, სადაცა" უბრმანა მათ იესუ. " და“ ვითარცა იხილეს იგი". თაყუანის სცეს მას და" რომელნი მე'" შეორგულდეს: " და!!! მოუგდა მათ იესუ. ეტყოდა მათ და ჰრქუა: მოცემელ არს ჩემდა!" ყოველი გელმწიფებაი ცათა შინა და ქუეყანასა ზედა ("+ ვითარცა მომავლინა მე მამამან, მეცა წარგავლინებ თქუენ'"· ” წარვედით და მოიმოწაფენით ყოველნი წარმართნი: და ნათელს "აცემდით მათ სახელითა მამისაითა და'' მისაითა და სულისა წმიდისაითა ” და ასწავებდით მათ დამარხვად ყოვლისა“ რაოდენი!" გამცენ" თქუენ. და აჰა ესერა მე თქუენ თანა ვარ ყოველთა დღეთა!” ვიდრე აღსასრუ- ლამდე სოფლისა ''"
way say that his disciples came by night and stole him while we were sleeping; and if that judge hears this, we will persuade him and make you secure." But they took that silver and did as they were instructed by them, and that word was spread among the Jews to this present day. Now those eleven disciples went into Galilee to that mountain where Jesus had commanded them; and when they saw him they worshiped him, but some doubted. And Jesus came to them, spoke to them, and said, "All authority has been given to me in heaven and on earth; as the Father sent me, I also send you; go and make disciples of all the Gentiles, and baptize them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit; and teach them to observe all things whatsoever I have commanded you. And behold, I am with you all the days until the consummation of the world.
